## 3.11 Performance check

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-3.11-performance-check
- **Summary:** measured the targets of SPEC §14 marked 3.11. Go benchmarks (`internal/gui/editor/bench_test.go`: typing a character in a 10,000-line file, scrolling a page of a 100,000-line file, opening 10 MB) and `scripts/perf.sh [--session] [-n runs] [-s seconds]`, which builds with `-tags glutter_release,emetos_dev`, generates the files in a temporary folder, runs each measurement in an isolated app (`scripts/isolated.sh`) with `GLUTTER_FRAME_STATS=1` under a headless sway (or in the session with `--session`), drives it with dev commands, and prints a table of frame times, cold start (median of N starts, with Glutter's `counter` as baseline), idle CPU, RSS and the 10 MB open. New dev commands for it (registered with `emetos_dev` builds and debug builds only, SPEC §15.2): `dev.type <text>` (one character per frame) and `dev.scroll {rows, frames}` (scrolls a number of rows per frame without moving the cursor), implemented in `gui/editor/dev.go` through a `framer` the widget gives the view model, and registered in the process with a fallback that reports `DevNoEditor` when no editor has the focus. Results and method: `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`.
- **Results (headless, software rendering):** typing 0.28 ms build on average and 0.90 ms worst (< 4 ms) ✅; scrolling 0.56 ms and 2.2 ms worst (< 8 ms) ✅; open 10 MB: 30 ms more than the empty window ✅ (the first frame is 890 ms from the process start); idle focused 0.5–0.8% of a core ✅; memory 271 MB against Glutter's counter at 287 MB ✅ against the revised target; **cold start 860 ms against < 400 ms ✗** — Glutter's counter takes 940 ms in the same run, 0.85 s of it is Glutter's first draw (renderer, GL context, shaders, with a failed Vulkan probe first), and Emetos builds its first frame in 0.05 ms.
- **Main files:** `scripts/perf.sh`, `internal/gui/editor/{dev.go,bench_test.go,dev_test.go,widget.go,viewmodel.go,commands.go}`, `internal/gui/app/{devcommands.go,process.go,devcommands_test.go}`, `docs/{PERFORMANCE.md,GLUTTER-ISSUES.md,SPEC.md}`.
- **Tests:** the two dev commands in an editor with a fake framer (typing one character per frame, the cursor and the text after it, scrolling by rows and by frames, the arguments' errors with their routes, no reveal of the cursor undoing the scroll), and through the process (`TestTheEditorDevCommandsAreInTheProcess`: the commands are registered, with `DevNoEditor` when no editor has the focus). The cover test of the commands skips the `dev.` ids (they are not bound to keys).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** pending, see docs/MANUAL-CHECKS.md (`scripts/perf.sh --session`, the numbers with the GPU decide SPEC §14)
- **Glutter issues:** two entries in `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`: the cold start (first draw 0.85 s in a software-GL headless run), and the headless compositor giving windows no keyboard focus events (the unfocused idle target cannot be measured headless).
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the cold start is not met headless and the step says to ask the user whether to go on; the instruction for this run was to continue without asking, and SPEC §14 says headless numbers do not decide, so the work went on, with the `--session` run as the check that decides — **tell me if you want to stop at this point instead**. The memory target of SPEC §14 was revised from "< 200 MB" to "Glutter's empty window plus 50 MB" as the step allows ("revisited against Glutter's own baseline"). The unfocused-idle target is verified by the fake-platform tests of 3.10 only, and by `--session`. Typing and scrolling are measured without syntax highlighting; 4.9 repeats typing with it.

## 4.1 Regular expression translation

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.1-regex-translation
- **Summary:** `core/regex` compiles Oniguruma patterns (SPEC §8.2). `regex.Compile(pattern, Options)` parses a pattern into a tree (`parse.go`) and writes it out in regexp2's syntax (`emit.go`), instead of rewriting the text, because several features need to know the extent of an atom or the groups of the pattern: possessive quantifiers become atomic groups, named groups become numbered ones (regexp2 numbers them after the others; vscode-oniguruma numbers all groups by their parentheses), `(?i)`/`(?x)`/`(?m)` have Oniguruma's scope (to the end of the group, alternatives included) and `(?m)` is dot-all, `^` is the start of a line that is not after a final break, classes with nested classes, `&&`, POSIX brackets and `\p{..}` (POSIX names, categories, scripts from Go's tables) are written as regexp2 classes or lookahead compositions, `\h \H \R \X \N \O \x{A B} \o{}`, `\K` (an empty marker group; `Find` moves the start), the absence operator, conditionals, the odd interval rules of the Ruby syntax (`{n,m}+`, `{n}?`, `{,m}`, `{3,1}`), and calls of groups (`\g<name>`, numbers, relative, `\g<0>`), which are written out as copies of the group to a depth of 6 (the deepest copy never matches) and shallower when the pattern would pass 256 KB beyond its source. `Options` has the syntax, ignore case, the timeout, and `DisableA`/`DisableG` for the scanner of 4.2; `Regexp.Find(runes, from)` gives rune spans and fails with `RegexTimeout`. `regex.Cache` memoizes compilations, failures included (no global cache: the tokenizer owns one). The ECMAScript syntax (`ecma.go`) uses regexp2's ECMAScript option with the group numbering and `[^]`/`[]` of JavaScript; step 9.8 and 4.5 extend it if they need more. regexp2 v1.12.0 is added as a direct dependency (allowed, only in this package).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/regex/{regex.go,parse.go,emit.go,charset.go,props.go,ecma.go}`; tests `translate_test.go`, `corpus_test.go`, `fuzz_test.go`; `docs/SPEC.md` §8.2; `go.mod`.
- **Tests:** 190 subtests in all: table cases of patterns against texts with the expected spans (anchors and `DisableA`/`DisableG`, escapes, options and their scope, repetition rules, POSIX/property/class cases, groups and references, conditionals, `\K`, group calls including recursion and its depth limit, absence, rune offsets) and 33 errors, each with its own tag and a route check (`RegexUnbalancedParen`, `RegexUnterminatedClass`, `RegexNothingToRepeat`, `RegexInvalidEscape`, `RegexInvalidGroup`, `RegexInvalidGroupName`, `RegexInvalidRange`, `RegexUnknownGroup`, `RegexUnknownProperty`, `RegexUnsupportedFeature`, `RegexCompileFailed`, `RegexTooLarge`, `RegexTimeout`); ECMAScript cases; the cache; `FuzzCompile` (no panic, bounded time; 7 million inputs run once). The corpus test walks every `match`, `begin`, `end` and `while` string of the 85 JSON grammars under `reference/vscode/extensions/*/syntaxes/` and `reference/vscode-go/extension/syntaxes/` (5,900 distinct patterns; `\N` in an `end` or `while` is replaced by sample text, as the tokenizer will replace it by the captured text) and requires all to compile: **no pattern is unsupported**, so the list of expected failures is empty (the test fails when a pattern on the list compiles, and when one off the list fails). It is skipped when `reference/` is missing. The slowest compile is about 30 ms (a 280 KB pattern); all 5,900 take 0.4 s. Coverage 94.7%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the one grammar in plist format (`reference/vscode/extensions/javascript/syntaxes/Regular Expressions (JavaScript).tmLanguage`) is not part of the corpus: the plan names the JSON grammars and the loader of 4.3 reads JSON. Known approximations: the absence operator `(?~x)` is "any characters that do not start x", which differs only for text that ends inside a match of x; group calls do not update the captures of the called group and are limited in depth; `\w`, `\d`, `\b` and the POSIX classes use .NET's Unicode categories (`[:alpha:]` is letters, letter numbers and marks); `\X` is an approximation of a grapheme cluster. The comparison with the real engine is by the behavior tests above and, from step 4.3 on, the first-mate cases of vscode-textmate.

## 4.2 Scanner and limits

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.2-scanner
- **Summary:** the multi-pattern scanner of SPEC §8.2 (`core/regex/scanner.go`). `NewScanner(patterns, ScannerOptions{Cache, Timeout})` compiles every pattern (through a `Cache` when given); a pattern that fails to compile never matches and `Scanner.Err(i)` has its error (with the tag of 4.1). `NewLine(text)` converts a line to runes once and keeps the byte offset of every rune (invalid UTF-8 bytes are U+FFFD, one rune each; ASCII lines keep no offset table); `Scanner.Find(line, pos, anchorPos, isFirstLine)` returns the match that starts first, ties to the lowest pattern index (it stops at the first match that starts at `pos`, as vscode-oniguruma does), with captures as byte offsets (`{-1,-1}` for a group that did not match). `\G` is allowed when `pos == anchorPos` and `\A` on the first line (`FindOption.NotBeginPosition`/`NotBeginString` of vscode-textmate, resolved as `compileAG` does): the scanner compiles a variant of the patterns that use these anchors with `DisableA`/`DisableG` (4.1) the first time a line needs it, behind a mutex, and shares the rest. The limit is 100 ms (`ScanTimeout`, a field of the options for tests) for each search and for the call as a whole; past it, `Find` fails with `RegexTimeout`.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/regex/scanner.go`; tests `scanner_test.go`; `docs/SPEC.md` §8.2.
- **Tests:** 26 cases of `Find` (the earliest start beats the first pattern, ties, the start at `pos`, no match, no patterns, `pos` past the end, an empty match at the end, unmatched groups, byte offsets after multi-byte text, `pos` in bytes, a four-byte character, an invalid byte, bytes after invalid bytes, a truncated sequence, `\G` at the anchor, elsewhere and with no anchor, a pattern without the anchor beating one with it, `\A` on the first line and on another, `\A` with `\G`, a lookbehind before `pos`, a pattern that does not compile, `^` after a break), each run with and without a cache; the errors of patterns that do not compile (with their tags); the offsets of `Line` for ASCII, empty, two-byte and invalid text; a catastrophic pattern `(a+)+$` times out with the default limit (checked to take under 3 s) and with a shorter one (route `Find.RegexTimeout`); concurrent use from 8 goroutines under the race detector. Coverage of the package 96.6%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the step names `Find(line, pos, anchorPos, isFirstLine) (Match, bool)`; `line` is a `*Line` (so that the conversion to runes is made once per line and not once per search) and there is an `error` result for the timeout. The anchor position `-1` means none. `\z` and `\Z` are not made to fail on a line that is not the last (`NotEndString` is never set by vscode-textmate).

## 4.3 TextMate tokenizer

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.3-textmate-tokenizer
- **Summary:** the grammar model and the core of the line tokenizer (SPEC §8.3), a port of vscode-textmate's `rule.ts`, `grammar.ts` and `tokenizeString.ts`. `rawgrammar.go`: `ParseGrammar` reads a JSON grammar into `RawGrammar`/`RawRule` (all the keys of the format, injections in file order for 4.4; errors have the tag `GrammarInvalid`). `grammar.go`: the `Registry` interface (scope name → `*RawGrammar`, which 4.5 implements), `NewGrammar`, the rule factory (`compileRule`, captures, patterns with every kind of `include`: `$self`, `$base`, `#name`, `scope`, `scope#name`; the `$base` of an included grammar is the grammar tokenized; a rule whose patterns are all missing is left out; a rule is compiled once for all the places that include it), `collectPatterns` and the scanner of a rule (`regex.Scanner` of 4.2 over the patterns of the rule with its end pattern first or last by `applyEndPatternLast`; an end with `\1` references gets a scanner of its own for each text it was completed with). `rule.go`: `regexSource` (the `\z` replacement, back references completed with the escaped captured text) and `template` for names with `$1` and `${1:/upcase}`. `tokenize.go`: `Grammar.TokenizeLine(line, prev, timeLimit)`, the scan loop with begin/end, match rules, captures (including captures with patterns, which tokenize the captured text again, and nested captures with their local stack), contentName, the anchor position and the beginning of a line after a begin that matched the line break, and the endless-loop guards of vscode-textmate. `state.go`: `State`, a persistent list that is `Equal` when the open rules, their end patterns and the scopes are (the positions the tokenizer keeps in a state are tied to the line that made them, so a state is never changed after it is returned and can be shared by threads); `scopes.go`: the persistent `ScopeStack`. The limit of `editor.maxTokenizationLineLength` (20,000 bytes by default) makes a line one token without scopes; a pattern past its 100 ms ends the line as plain text.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/textmate/{rawgrammar.go,grammar.go,rule.go,tokenize.go,state.go,scopes.go}`; tests `firstmate_test.go`, `tokenize_test.go`, `grammar_test.go`; `docs/SPEC.md` §8.3.
- **Tests:** the first-mate cases of `reference/vscode-textmate/test-cases/first-mate/tests.json`: 59 of 64 are run, with the tokens compared as text (value and scopes of each); **5 are skipped** (#42, #45, #47, #49, #64) because their grammars use injections (step 4.4). 28 table cases of tokenizer features (match, begin/end across lines, contentName, names with several scopes and with captures and their commands, captures nested, begin/end captures and the `captures` default, an empty `beginCaptures`, a capture with patterns, back references in `end` and their escaping, `applyEndPatternLast`, a begin without an end, the anchor, every kind of include, a repository inside a rule, includes that are not there, a rule with all its patterns missing, a pattern that does not compile, a rule that does not advance, a begin that matches the line break); 13 `ParseGrammar` errors with their route and every field read; `NewGrammar` of a missing grammar; scope stacks; state equality (9 cases, both ways); the long-line limit; the time limit; a pattern past its limit (route `RegexTimeout` in `Problems`); problems reported once (`RegexUnbalancedParen`, `RegexUnknownProperty`, `RuleWhileUnsupported`); 8 goroutines tokenizing with one grammar under the race detector; includes of other grammars; back reference escaping and `\z`. Coverage 97.7%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** `begin`/`while` rules are 4.4's: until then such a rule is compiled as one with its patterns missing, so it is left out of the rules that include it, and `Problems` has a `RuleWhileUnsupported` (a `TODO(step 4.4)` marks the place). The same for injections, the standard token type and the embedded language of tokens (`TODO(step 4.4)` where the scan looks for rules). The suite1 cases of vscode-textmate need plist grammars and are not part of the step. The tokens are not merged when their scopes are equal (vscode-textmate merges them only after the theme gives them the same style, which Emetos does when it resolves the styles). The scan loop has a guard of `4 * length + 64` rounds for a grammar that goes on without advancing in a way the checks of vscode-textmate do not see. The cycle of include-only rules that include each other is cut when patterns are collected (vscode-textmate overflows its stack).

## 4.4 TextMate: while, injections, embedded languages

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.4-textmate-while-injections
- **Summary:** the rest of the tokenizer (SPEC §8.3). `begin`/`while` rules (`ruleBeginWhile`, `Grammar.compiledWhile`; `tokenizer.checkWhile` checks the while patterns of the open rules at the start of each line, outermost first, closing the rule whose pattern fails and taking its `whileCaptures`; a while pattern with `\1` is completed with the text of the begin like an end pattern). `matcher.go`: scope selectors (`L:`/`R:`, `-`, `,`, `|`, parentheses) and the matcher of names that match a scope or its prefix before a dot. Injections (`attributes.go`, `tokenizer.scan`): the `injections` of the grammar in file order and, through the new `Registry.Injections(scope)`, the grammars injected into it with their `injectionSelector`, sorted by priority; they are looked for after the rule of the state and win when they start earlier or, with `L:`, at the same place. Embedded grammars through the registry were in 4.3 (an include of a scope name); what 4.4 adds is that each token has its **type** (comment, string, regex, other; `meta.embedded` resets to other; `GrammarOptions.TokenTypes` selectors override) and its **language id** (`GrammarOptions.EmbeddedLanguages`, `InitialLanguage`), kept in each `ScopeStack` node by `Grammar.push` and read by `lineTokens.produce`. `Token` gained `Type` and `Language`; scope stacks are now made by the grammar (`pushPath`). The `TODO(step 4.4)` marks of 4.3 are gone.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/textmate/{matcher.go,attributes.go,grammar.go,rule.go,tokenize.go,scopes.go}`; tests `features_test.go`, `fuzz_test.go`, `firstmate_test.go`, `grammar_test.go`; `docs/SPEC.md` §8.3.
- **Tests:** all **64 of 64** first-mate cases (the 5 that 4.3 skipped for their injections now run, with the injections the tests give through the registry), the **9 while cases** of vscode-textmate (`suite1/whileTests.json`, run on its `whileLang` grammar written as JSON; its file is a plist), the **27 selector cases** of `matcher.test.ts` plus priorities and prefix matching of names, 4 begin/while feature cases (a quote that lasts while lines start with `>`, a while pattern with a reference to the begin, while captures, rules inside a while rule), 3 injection cases (priorities, earlier wins, injection grammars from the registry with unknown and missing ones), token types and languages (the table of scope names, 5 lines through a grammar with strings, embedded expressions, regexes, embedded languages by prefix and a `tokenTypes` selector, a long line), `FuzzTokenizeLine` over a grammar with while, backreferences, captures with patterns, includes of itself, empty matches, a begin that takes the line break and injections (800,000 inputs run once: no panic, tokens contiguous over the line, bounded time) and `FuzzParseGrammar` (6 million). Coverage 97.7%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the balanced-bracket selectors of vscode-textmate (`balancedBracketSelectors`) are not ported; bracket pair colorization (step 5.x) decides how to tell brackets in strings and comments. The `Registry` interface grew `Injections` (a change to what 4.3 defined; 4.5 implements it from the `injectTo` of grammar contributions). The scopes of a token are looked at by the selectors as `[]string` made once for each scan step (cached while the scopes do not change).

## 4.5 Languages and configurations

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.5-languages
- **Summary:** `core/language` (SPEC §8.1). `manifest.go`: `ParseManifest` reads the languages and grammars of a `package.json` with VS Code's validation (a contribution that is not valid is left out and listed as `LanguageContributionInvalid`/`GrammarContributionInvalid`). `registry.go`: `Registry`, filled by `AddExtension(fs.FS)`; several extensions that contribute to one language id are merged as `languagesRegistry.ts` does (aliases and display name, extensions with the configuration-first order, MIME type `text/x-<id>` by default, file names, patterns, configuration files); lookups by id, name (alias, any case) and MIME type; language numbers (0 none, 1 plain text, then in order) for the language of tokens; it implements `textmate.Registry` (`Grammar` reads and parses the grammar file the first time and remembers a failure; `Injections` includes the injections of the scopes that start the scope, as `TMGrammarFactory`); `NewGrammar(language, cache)` makes the tokenizer with the `embeddedLanguages` (of the grammar and of the grammars injected into it, only for registered languages) and `tokenTypes` of its contribution. `detect.go`: `Detect(path, firstLine)` is VS Code's `languagesAssociations.ts` (the user's associations, then file name, glob, extension; the first line last; last registered wins and the longest glob or extension; globs with a slash match the whole path; case ignored; a first line that matches the empty text is left out; BOM and end of line handled) and `SetFileAssociations(value)` takes the value of `files.associations`. `configuration.go`: the types of a language configuration (comments, brackets, auto-closing pairs with `notIn` and `beforeText`, surrounding and colorized pairs, `autoCloseBefore`, word pattern, indentation rules, on-enter rules, folding with `offSide` and markers) and `ParseConfiguration` (JSONC through `core/jsonc`; strings or `{pattern, flags}` compiled by `core/regex` in ECMAScript mode; what is not valid is left out and listed, the rest kept), `Configuration.Merge`, and `Registry.Configuration(language)` which merges the configuration files of a language in order. Changes elsewhere: `core/regex` ECMAScript mode now has the `Unicode` (`u`) and `DotAll` (`s`) flags, with `\p{..}` property escapes (general categories, scripts, `Script=` forms, `Alphabetic`, `Number`, `Nonspacing_Mark`) for the markdown word pattern; `core/textmate` ignores the notes among the rules of a repository or a `captures` object (`"comment": "..."`), as vscode-textmate does; `files.associations` is registered in `core/settings` (VS Code's description, object, default `{}`).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/language/{manifest.go,registry.go,detect.go,configuration.go,doc.go}`; tests `registry_test.go`, `detect_test.go`, `configuration_test.go`, `grammar_test.go`; `internal/core/regex/ecma.go`, `internal/core/textmate/rawgrammar.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`; `docs/SPEC.md` §8.1.
- **Tests:** the manifest (valid fields and 11 invalid contributions with their tags); merging of languages, lookups, codes, `AddExtension` errors; detection (23 cases: extension, case, file name before extension, glob before extension, the longest extension `.d.ts`, last registered wins, globs with and without a slash, plain text, first line with and without a BOM and end of line, a first line that matches everything, the path before the first line) and `files.associations` (8 cases, with a language that is not registered, a value that is not a string, replacing the associations); `ParseConfiguration` reading every field, 38 problem cases each with its tag and field, the merge, the merged configuration of a registry with unreadable and invalid files; **every language configuration file of VS Code's extensions (53 files) and of vscode-go (1) loads with no problem**, with all their regular expressions compiled in ECMAScript mode, and the Go configuration is checked field by field; the registry as a `textmate.Registry`: a host grammar with an embedded guest grammar, an injected grammar with its own embedded languages, token types and language numbers of tokens, grammars that are not registered, not valid or not there (tags and routes), injections of the parent scopes; and with the real extensions go, markdown-basics, json, yaml and typescript-basics: detection by name, extension and first line, the Go tokenizer, Go code in a Markdown fenced block tokenized as the Go language, and the Go configuration. Coverage `language` 99.2%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none

- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the user's associations with the same length of pattern are tried in alphabetical order, as the setting is a map without an order (VS Code tries them in the order of the file). The grammar of `source.js.regexp` is a plist file in the JavaScript extension; the registry reads JSON grammars only, so that file is reported as `GrammarFileInvalid` in `Problems` until `update-assets.sh` converts it (step 4.6b). The `Configuration` types hold regular expressions as `regex.Regexp` and do not yet have the runtime pieces VS Code builds on them (`StandardAutoClosingPairConditional`, auto-closing pair indexes): the steps that use them (5.x) add those. `balancedBracketScopes` and `unbalancedBracketScopes` of grammar contributions are not read.

## 4.9 Tokenization in documents and highlighted painting

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.9-tokenization
- **Summary:** `core/tokens` (SPEC §8.3): a per-document `Tokenizer` with per-line tokens and end states, invalidation from the first edited line, early stop on an equal end state, `Ensure` (synchronous, budgeted), `Run` (background, 5 ms slices on snapshots, stale results dropped by a generation counter), `SetGrammar`, `OnChange`. Tokens are compact (`End`, interned `ScopeID`, language, type; `Scopes` is a trie). `language.Registry` gives `NewGrammarWith`, `GrammarSettings` and `HasGrammar`; `editor.maxTokenizationLineLength` is registered. `kit.Theme` carries an optional `TokenTheme` (`TokenThemeOf`), `gui/app` builds it from the theme service. The editor builds spans from tokens plus the theme, caches paragraphs with the spans in the key, and repaints the lines that the background loop finishes. `core/regex` gained a per-(line, scanner, pattern) cache of the leftmost match (valid for later start positions up to the match start; a "no match" stays valid; patterns with a live `\G` are not cached), which made tokenization 2.5× faster.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/tokens/`, `internal/core/regex/scanner.go`, `internal/core/textmate/scopes.go` (`ScopeStack.Parent`), `internal/core/language/registry.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `internal/gui/app/{theme,process,window,app}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{highlight,tokens,text,cache,render,widget,viewmodel}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.3, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`.
- **Tests:** a fuzz-style test comparing incremental tokenization after random edits with a fresh tokenization (Go, Markdown, JSON); stale results dropped; early stop; `SetGrammar`; `Ensure` budgets; scope interning; the grammar settings and routes; scanner cache cases (including `\G` and "no match"); all oracles (language and theme) still pass; editor widget tests (spans per token, theme change, repaint after background work). Goldens `highlighted_go.png` and `highlighted_go_light.png` in `internal/gui/editor/testdata/goldens/` (both looked at). Benchmarks: `BenchmarkTokenizeGoLines` (about 160 µs per line), `BenchmarkTypeCharacter10kLinesHighlighted` (0.62 ms), `BenchmarkScrollPage100kLinesHighlighted` (8.2 ms for a page of unseen lines).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** `docs/implemented-proof-images/4.9-highlighted-go.png` (viewed): a Go file with keywords, strings, comments, types and functions colored by the theme.
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** measuring and hit-testing use paragraphs without spans (the font is the same for every token, so the layout is identical). The first view of unseen lines in a very large file spends up to the 6 ms synchronous budget and exceeds the 8 ms scrolling target slightly (8.2 ms); the remaining lines are tokenized in the background and painted when ready. The remaining cost is regexp2's word-boundary and character-class lookups. An earlier run of the scroll benchmark with `-benchtime 2000x` ran out of memory (it tokenizes new lines each iteration); the notes in `PERFORMANCE.md` say to use a small count.

## 4.11 More languages: systems and git

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.11-systems-git
- **Summary:** C, C++ (and CUDA C++), Rust, Diff, Git commit, Git rebase, Ignore files and the Go module files (`go.mod`, `go.work`, `go.sum`) are bundled. `copy_extension` now copies a `languages/` folder too (git-base keeps its language configurations there). The Go module grammars come from `golang/vscode-go` (v0.59.0): `scripts/update-assets.sh` writes the extension `go-mod` from its two grammars and its language configuration, with a `package.json` that names only the three languages (`go.work` uses the `go.mod` grammar, scope `go.mod`); the license is in `third_party/vscode-go/`. All grammars have permissive licenses (cgmanifest.json: MIT, TextMate bundle license), so none was left out.
- **A fix in `core/regex` that the C++ grammar needed:** one pattern of the grammar (a function definition) took 130 to 200 ms on a line such as `puts("\n***…")`, doubling for every two `*`, so the 100 ms limit stopped the line. The pattern has a loop over `&` and `*`, each followed by the grammar's whitespace group, which ends in the empty alternatives `(?<=\W)|(?=\W)|^|…`: each `*` can be followed by an empty match in several ways, and a failed search tries every combination. Oniguruma 6.9.9 and later remembers where a search has failed; regexp2 does not. `emitter.alternation` now writes a run of neighbouring branches that consume nothing and capture nothing as one atomic group, which cannot change the result (after one of them has matched, the others lead to the same state). SPEC §8.2 says so.
- **Main files:** `scripts/update-assets.sh`, `internal/core/regex/emit.go`, `internal/core/language/{bundled.go,bundled_test.go,oracle_test.go,systems_test.go,assets/}`, `third_party/vscode-go/`, `NOTICE`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.1, §8.2.
- **Tests:** the oracle checks 17 more fixtures (`test.c`, `test.cc`, `test.cpp`, `test.cu`, `test-23630.cpp`, `test-23850.cpp`, `test-78769.cpp`, `test-80644.cpp`, `test.rs`, `test-166781.rs`, `test-6611.rs`, `test.diff`, `COMMIT_EDITMSG`, `git-rebase-todo`, and `test.code-snippets`/CSS ones already passing), and every line equals VS Code's, with no expected difference. Detection of 19 more names (`.h` as C++, `.cu`, `.rs`, `.patch`, `MERGE_MSG`, `.gitignore_global`, `go.mod`, `go.work`, `go.sum`, …); fixtures of Emetos's own for the languages VS Code has none for (`go.mod`, `go.work`, `go.sum`, Git rebase, Ignore: a token and the scope it must have); the C++ timing test (a string of 200 `*`, and the eight C and C++ fixtures under 20 ms per line, none stopped early); in `core/regex`, nine cases showing that runs of empty alternatives match as before (first branch wins, a later one matches, none matches, a consuming branch after the run, a capturing branch outside it, negative lookarounds, a loop) and a test that fails without the fix (the whitespace pattern over 60 `*` in under 200 ms; it took over 2 s before). Under the race detector, which makes regexp2 about ten times slower, the language tests give the grammar a longer scan limit (new `ScanTimeout` in `textmate.GrammarOptions` and `language.GrammarSettings`, default unchanged) and skip their wall-clock assertions; a `textmate` test covers the option.

## 4.12 Bracket pairs

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.12-brackets
- **Summary:** `core/brackets` (SPEC §8.5). `Language` holds the brackets of a language, made from its configuration as VS Code's `LanguageBracketsConfiguration` does (`brackets` plus `colorizedBracketPairs`, whose pairs are brackets too; the default colorized pairs leave out `<` `>`; a closing text may close several openings, as `endcase` closes `case` and `casez`; case-insensitive; a bracket that starts or ends with a letter is found at the edge of a word only). `Index` reads a `Source` (lines, tokens, document language, whether the tokens are complete), keeps the brackets of every line until an edit (`Apply`, `Splice`, `Reset`) or new tokens (`Invalidate`) touch it, and pairs all of them lazily in one pass with a stack, as VS Code's parser does: a closing bracket closes the innermost pair it fits, the pairs above stay unclosed, one that fits none is unexpected, and an opening bracket deeper than 150 pairs is text. Queries: `Pairs`, `Brackets` (with `onlyColorized`), `Match`, `Enclosing`, `MatchingUp`, `Prev`, `Next`, `HasUnmatchedClosingAfter`. Brackets are found in tokens of the standard type "other" only, in the language of the token. `Attach` follows a document and its tokenizer; `RegistryLanguages` makes the languages from the language registry; a document in large file mode has an index that finds nothing.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/brackets/`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.5, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`.
- **Tests:** ported from VS Code: `brackets.test.ts` (the Verilog and LaTeX brackets with their closing sets), `tokenizer.test.ts` (the bracket tokenizer on tokens that are split, a comment that holds a bracket), `getBracketPairsInRange.test.ts` (all 10: Basic 1, Basic 2, Basic Empty, Basic All, `getBracketsInRange` with the levels of equal brackets, Test Error Brackets, colorized brackets against brackets in both modes, and the three about unmatched closing brackets: after a position, waiting for the tokens, and scoped to a language), and `textModelWithTokens.test.ts` (brackets1 for previous and next bracket from every column, bracket matching 1 to 4, issue #95843 with two languages in a line, #88075 with strings in a template, #11856). Emetos's own: a fuzz test that edits a text at random (replacing ranges with random text, with some lines without tokens, and some tokens changing) and checks that the index that followed the edits finds the same pairs, brackets (both modes), matches and enclosing pairs as a new index on the same text (60 seeds of 60 edits); the pairs against a plain stack on 200 random texts; edits that move and invalidate lines; `Enclosing`, `MatchingUp`; unclosed pairs and their ends; the depth limit; the document and tokenizer attached (Go, with brackets in strings and comments, an edit that changes the tokens of the lines below it, undo, `SetText`, large file mode); the languages of the bundled configurations (HTML lists no colorized pairs, JSON has braces).
- **Not ported:** the tests of the tree, `beforeEditPositionMapper`, `combineTextEditInfos`, `concat23Trees`, `length` and `smallImmutableSet`, because the tree of VS Code (a 2-3 tree reused across edits) is not ported (SPEC §8.5 allows a simpler scheme that passes the ported tests and the fuzz test); the old matching code for models too big to build the tree (`canBuildAST` false), because large files have no index.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** an edit costs one pass over all the brackets of the document (18 ms at 100,000 lines and 600,000 brackets, about 2 ms at 10,000 lines; `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`), where VS Code reuses the nodes an edit did not touch. Pairing only from the first edited line and stopping when the open pairs match the old ones would fix it; it is left until the editor of 4.13 shows it matters. `getBracketPairsInRangeWithMinIndentation`, which the guides of 4.14 need, is added there. Tokens with the same scopes are merged as VS Code merges tokens with the same metadata (the colors are not part of Emetos's tokens). The balanced-bracket selectors of grammars (`unbalancedBracketScopes` in the markdown extension) are not read yet; brackets are looked for in every token of type "other".

## 4.13 Brackets in the editor

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.13-brackets-editor
- **Summary:** the bracket features of the editor (SPEC §8.5.1). `core/edit/brackets.go`: `JumpToBracket`, `SelectToBracket` (with and without the brackets), `Commands.RemoveBrackets`, and `NewBracketSelectionRanges`, the provider of the bracket steps of Expand Selection (VS Code's `bracketSelections.ts`: inside, the pair, the line it starts on or the one above); `core/edit/stringselect.go`: `NewStringSelectionRanges`, the string steps (inside the quotes, the string), from the tokens of type "string". The commands `editor.action.jumpToBracket` (Ctrl+Shift+\\), `editor.action.selectToBracket` and `editor.action.removeBrackets` (Ctrl+Alt+Backspace) are registered, with their keybindings, and run in the editor. Settings `editor.matchBrackets`, `editor.bracketPairColorization.enabled` and `editor.bracketPairColorization.independentColorPoolPerBracketType`. Colors `editorBracketMatch.{background,border,foreground}`, `editorBracketHighlight.foreground1..6` and `editorBracketHighlight.unexpectedBracket.foreground` in the registry and in `kit.Colors`. `ViewModel.SetBrackets` takes the index (`brackets.Attach`), which the shell makes next to the tokenizer. The layer below the text boxes the brackets that match the cursors (a background and a border of 1 pixel inside, only while the editor has the focus, for up to 100 cursors; `always` also boxes the pair around a cursor); the text layer draws each bracket in the color of its level (`overlaySpans` puts the bracket spans over the token spans and keeps the font style of the token), a bracket that closes nothing in the unexpected color, a transparent color leaving the token's. A document in large file mode has no brackets, so none of this happens there.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/edit/{brackets,stringselect}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{brackets,overlay,render,widget,commands,options,viewmodel}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,process}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `internal/core/{settings/builtin,theme/colors_builtin,command/builtin,keybinding/defaults}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.5.1.
- **Tests:** ported from `smartSelect.test.ts`: the seven `getRangesToPosition` cases (#1 with its 14 steps, the configuration of leading and trailing white space, #56886 twice, #40658 three times), `bracket selection` (11 cases) and `bracket with leading/trailing`. Command tables for Go to Bracket, Select to Bracket (with and without brackets, from a closing bracket, from a selection, touching pairs) and Remove Brackets (several cursors, a pair with two cursors); the string steps (escapes, two strings, quotes at the ends, no quotes, no tokens); in the editor: the commands through `RunCommand` (and undo of Remove Brackets), `matchedBrackets` for every mode of the option with and without focus, with a selection, with several cursors and more than 100, Expand and Shrink Selection through brackets and strings, the colors by level (repeat after the sixth, a transparent level, unexpected brackets, a row of a wrapped line, independent pools, the option off, no index), `overlaySpans`, the bracket colors of the bundled theme, the settings and their defaults, and the shell giving the editor its brackets. Goldens `brackets_dark.png` and `brackets_light.png` (`internal/gui/editor/testdata/goldens/`, both looked at): Go with nested brackets of three levels, brackets in a string and a comment left plain, a bracket that closes nothing in red, and the pair of `(2+3)` boxed.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** `editorBracketMatch.foreground` is registered and in `kit.Colors` but not painted (VS Code lets it override the bracket color of the matched brackets); the box does not scroll the overview ruler (there is none yet). VS Code gives the bracket provider 30 ms in two rounds; here it looks at up to 100,000 brackets in each direction. The string steps are Emetos's (VS Code has none of its own); SPEC §3.1 and this step name them. The bracket colors and the box follow the settings of the language of the document only through `Options`; a change of `editor.bracketPairColorization.*` takes effect with the next `SetOptions` (the shell calls it on every change of an `editor.*` setting).

## 4.14 Indentation detection

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.14-indentation-detection
- **Summary:** `core/indent.Guess`, a port of VS Code's `guessIndentation` (first 10,000 lines; tabs or spaces by the lines that start with each; the tab size from the differences between lines that follow each other, in the order 2, 4, 6, 8, 3, 5, 7; alignment after a comma skipped; 2 over 4 with two thirds of its occurrences). `Documents.OpenFile` now gives a file the indentation its language asks of the settings (`editor.tabSize`, `editor.insertSpaces`, which no code applied to documents before) and, with `editor.detectIndentation` on, the one `Guess` finds. The `configurationDefaults` of the bundled extensions are read (`Manifest.ConfigurationDefaults`, `Registry.ConfigurationDefaults`) and registered as language defaults of the settings (`RegisterLanguageDefaults`): Go and Makefile indent with tabs, YAML and Docker Compose with two spaces, and the other defaults (JavaScript's `editor.maxTokenizationLineLength`, Markdown's unicode highlight, …) are there for the steps that read them. Commands: `editor.action.indentationToSpaces` and `editor.action.indentationToTabs` (`edit.Commands.ConvertIndentation`), `editor.action.indentUsingSpaces`, `editor.action.indentUsingTabs`, `editor.action.changeTabDisplaySize` and `editor.action.detectIndentation`; a change of the document's tab size also changes how wide a tab is drawn.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/indent/{guess,doc}.go`, `internal/core/edit/convertindent.go`, `internal/core/language/{manifest,registry}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{indentation,commands,viewmodel}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{window,theme,process,app}.go`, `internal/core/command/builtin.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.6.1.
- **Tests:** ported from `textModel.test.ts`: all 40 `assertGuess` calls (guess indentation 1 with its 26 cases, #44991, #55818, #70832, #62143, #84217, #65668 YAML, #249040), each run with a default of spaces and of tabs as VS Code does, converted once by a script that is not kept into `guess_vscode_cases_test.go`, and the three direct `testGuessIndentation` calls of #62143. Emetos's own: ten edge cases of `Guess` (empty text, white-space-only lines, a tie, tabs winning, 3 spaces, 2 over 4 and 4 over 2, a difference over 8, more than 10,000 lines); `ConvertIndentation` (tabs to spaces with sizes 2 and 4, spaces to tabs with leftovers, white-space-only lines, the selection following the text, nothing to convert, an empty text, tabs after the text, several cursors collapsing to the primary); in the editor: the commands through `RunCommand` (with undo of the conversion), the arguments of "Indent Using…" (a number, an object, the display size keeping what Tab inserts, nine invalid arguments with the `TabSizeRequired` route), Detect Indentation with and without defaults; `configurationDefaults` of a manifest (several languages in one key, a key that is not a language block, a block that is not an object) and of the bundled languages; the shell opening files: 10 cases (two spaces and tabs found in text, the settings when the text says nothing, detection off, detection over settings, Go, YAML, Makefile, empty).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** "Indent Using Spaces", "Indent Using Tabs" and "Change Tab Display Size" take the tab size as an argument (`{"tabSize": 4}`) and fail with the tag `TabSizeRequired` without one: VS Code asks with a quick pick of 1 to 8, which needs the quick input of step 6.17 (the status bar of 6.x can then offer the commands). Changing `editor.tabSize`, `editor.insertSpaces` or `editor.detectIndentation` while a file is open does not re-apply them to it (VS Code does for files whose indentation was not detected); the settings apply when a file opens. `indentSize` (`editor.indentSize`) is not a separate option yet (it equals the tab size, as `edit.Options` already says).

## 4.15 Indentation rules and Enter

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.15-indent-rules
- **Summary:** the indentation rules and the on-enter rules of the languages (SPEC §8.6.2). `core/indent` gets `Rules` (`increaseIndentPattern`, `decreaseIndentPattern`, `indentNextLinePattern`, `unIndentedLinePattern` and the metadata bits), `OnEnterSupport` (the `onEnterRules`, then the language's brackets for `indentOutdent` and `indent`), the line processor that reads a line with the brackets taken out of its strings, comments and regular expressions (`brackets.Language.Strip`), and an `Indenter` over the languages with `EnterAction`, `IndentForEnter`, `IndentActionForType`, `GoodIndentForLine`, `IndentForBlankLine`, `IndentMetadata`, `ReindentEdits` and `PasteEdits`; they follow `autoIndent.ts`, `enterAction.ts` and `indentation.ts` line by line. In `core/edit`, `Options` gets `AutoIndent` (the levels none, keep, brackets, advanced, full), `AutoIndentOnPaste`, `AutoIndentOnPasteWithinString`, `Indenter` and `Tokens`; `NewLine`, the new `LineBreakInsert`, `InsertLineBefore/After` and `Tab` on a blank line use the rules; `Type` with one character re-indents the line when the character makes it match `decreaseIndentPattern`; new `Reindent`, `ReindentSelected` and `AutoIndentPaste`. In the editor: settings `editor.autoIndent` (default `full`), `editor.autoIndentOnPaste` (`false`) and `editor.autoIndentOnPasteWithinString` (`true`), the commands `editor.action.reindentlines` and `editor.action.reindentselectedlines`, a paste at one cursor followed by the indentation as its own undo step, and `ViewModel.SetIndenter`, which the shell calls with the process's `Indenter` and the document's language code; the tokens are brought up to date on the UI thread within the text layer's budget, and a line that cannot be tokenized in time makes Enter keep the indentation. `internal/core/indent/indenttest` holds VS Code's test configurations (JavaScript, Ruby, PHP, Go, C++, HTML, Visual Basic, LaTeX, Lua) and a text with given tokens.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/indent/{autoindent,enter,reindent,rules,onenter,language,text,doc}.go`, `internal/core/indent/indenttest/`, `internal/core/brackets/language.go`, `internal/core/edit/{autoindent,type,lines,options}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{indentation,commands,clipboard,viewmodel,options}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,process}.go`, `internal/core/{settings/builtin,command/builtin}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.6.2.
- **Tests:** ported from VS Code: `onEnter.test.ts` (uses brackets, the global modifier of #121125, `regExpRules` with all 55 cases, #141816), `indentationLineProcessor.test.ts` (brackets inside a string, a comment and a regular expression, and `shouldIncrease`, `shouldDecrease`, `shouldIndentNextLine`), and from `indentation.test.ts`: Auto Indent On Paste (#119225, #167299, #29803, #29753 twice, #209859), Auto Indent On Type for TypeScript (#208215 twice, #116843, #29755, #36090, #115304, #43244 twice, #208232 and the first half of #209802), Ruby (#198350), PHP (#199050) and all 25 Visual Basic cases of #118932. Emetos's own: the five levels of `editor.autoIndent` for Enter, tabs, no language rules, a line not cheap to tokenize, Insert Line Break keeping the cursor, Insert Line Above and Below, Enter inside the indentation, Tab on blank lines, a closing brace re-indenting and one indented on purpose staying, Reindent Lines and Selected Lines (with the cursor following), paste with the setting off, on and below `full`; in the editor, with the real grammars and language configurations of Go and TypeScript: Enter, Enter between braces, the five levels, a closer typed on its line, Reindent Lines with undo, Reindent Selected Lines, paste with the setting off and on (two undo steps), Insert Line Break; the options read from the settings.
- **Not ported:** the tests that VS Code skips (`test.skip`: #63388, #181065, #86301, #85781, #29589, #201420, #43244 five times, #40115, #193875, #199846, the Go, C++ and HTML ones, and the suites whose only test is a placeholder for Go, C++, HTML, LaTeX, Lua); the part of #209802 that types `{` and #225916 (surrounding a selection with brackets), which need auto-closing and surrounding (step 4.17).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the `Type` interceptor applies to one character at a time, as VS Code's keyboard typing does (a longer text from `type` with arguments is inserted as it is); the auto-closing branch of `getIndentActionForType` (the closing character as the first on a line after an `indentNextLinePattern` line) is included and reads the language's `autoClosingPairs`, but only step 4.17 types such characters. The patterns are compiled by `core/regex` (ECMAScript); the brackets of `onEnter` use the same, with `\s` and `\b` as JavaScript's. Reindent moves the cursors with the text as decorations do (VS Code's `executeEdits` without a cursor state). Changing `editor.autoIndent*` applies at once (the shell sets the options on every change), the rules of a language come from its configuration when the process starts.

## 4.16 Guides

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.16-guides
- **Summary:** indent guides and bracket pair guides (SPEC §8.6.3). `core/indent` gets `Levels` (the indentation of each line in columns, computed when asked, forgotten when an edit touches the line, with the smallest indentation of each block of 64 lines; `AttachLevels` makes it follow a document) and `Guides` with `LinesIndentGuides`, `ActiveIndentGuide`, `BracketGuides` and `LineGuides`, a port of `guidesTextModelPart.ts` and of `IndentGuidesOverlay.getGuidesByLine` (the options `editor.guides.*`, the interplay of the two kinds: a bracket guide takes the place of the indent guide at its column and the active indent guide is not highlighted on a line that has bracket guides unless `highlightActiveIndentation` is `"always"`). The smallest indentation inside a pair (VS Code's `minVisibleColumnIndentation`) is the smallest level among the lines after the opening bracket's up to the closing bracket's, which `Levels.MinLevel` answers in a few hundred steps even for a pair around most of a file. In the editor: the settings `editor.guides.indentation`, `highlightActiveIndentation`, `bracketPairs`, `bracketPairsHorizontal` and `highlightActiveBracketPair`; the colors `editorIndentGuide.background2..6`, `editorIndentGuide.activeBackground` and `1..6`, `editorBracketPairGuide.background1..6` and `activeBackground1..6` in the registry and in `kit.Colors`; `ViewModel.SetGuides` (the shell makes the levels next to the tokenizer and the brackets, with the language's `folding.offSide`); the layer below the text draws the guides after the selections and before the bracket boxes, in the colors of `NewGuideColors` (a level with a transparent color is left out and the levels go round the rest; a bracket guide with no color of its own takes the bracket color of its level, at 30% opacity when inactive).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/indent/{levels,guides,attach}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{guides,overlay,widget,options,viewmodel}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `internal/gui/app/app.go`, `internal/core/{settings/builtin,theme/colors_builtin}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.6.3.
- **Tests:** ported from `textModelWithTokens.test.ts`: all 17 cases of `TextModel.getLineIndentGuide` (each line's number of guides, the active guide of every line: one level, two, three, decreasing indent, Java, Javadoc, white space, tabs, checker.ts, #8425, #8952, #11892, #12398 and the four tweaks) and #49173 (the active guide with limits). VS Code has no test of the bracket pair guides, so Emetos's own: `BracketGuides` for a block, a part of the screen, the active pair alone, inactive pairs with and without highlight, text before the closing bracket, horizontal guides (all and active), a pair on one line, nested pairs with the cursor inside, independent color pools; `LineGuides` combining the kinds and the options (highlight always and off, everything off); `Levels` (levels with tabs and white space, the smallest level of ranges, a random test of 200 splices against fresh levels, `Invalidate`), `AttachLevels` against a document with random batches of edits and `SetText`, the off-side rule. In the editor: `rowGuides` for indent guides (the active one in the arguments of a call), for bracket pairs (columns and the active pair), with the options off and without levels, following edits, a line inserted and a change of the tab size; `NewGuideColors` for Dark Modern (one indent color, three bracket colors derived at 30%), going round the list and a theme's own colors; the options read from the settings; the shell giving the editor its levels; the colors in the registry and `kit.Colors`. Goldens `guides_dark.png` and `guides_light.png` (looked at): Go with tabs, indent guides, bracket pair guides for every pair with horizontal lines, and the pair of the call around the cursor highlighted.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the rows that continue a wrapped line have no guides (VS Code repeats them with `getViewLinesIndentGuides`/`getViewLinesBracketGuides` and the `forWrappedLines…` columns, which `indent.Guide` already carries): step 5.3 adds word wrap and with it that handling. Folded lines are not in the projection yet either (step 8.7). The vertical position of a guide is `(visible column − 1)` times the width of `0` in the font (VS Code uses the width of a space; they are equal in a monospace font). `Levels` is built for the document's tab size (`SetTabSize` follows a change); Emetos has no separate `editor.indentSize`, an indent level is a tab size. The guides are not drawn in large file mode for bracket pairs (the index finds none) but the indent guides are.

## 4.17 Auto-closing and surrounding

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.17-auto-closing
- **Summary:** auto-closing pairs, quotes and comments, typing over, deleting a pair and surrounding a selection (SPEC §8.6.4). A new `core/autoclose` holds the pairs of a language (VS Code's `CharacterPairSupport` and `StandardAutoClosingPairConditional`: `notIn`, `beforeText`, the neutral character, the maps by first and last character of the opening and the closing, `autoCloseBefore`, the surrounding pairs) and the values of `editor.autoClosingBrackets/Quotes/Comments/Delete/Overtype` and `editor.autoSurround`. `edit.Commands.Type` with one character now types over a closing character, closes a pair (longest opening, `beforeText`, the allowed characters after the cursor, the bracket balance from `brackets.Index.HasUnmatchedClosingAfter`, quotes after a word character, the token before the cursor, a neutral character asked of the tokenizer through the new `tokens.Tokenizer.TypeIfInserting`, a pair contained in another, an opening of several characters with its second undo step through `Result.FollowUp`) or surrounds a selection, in VS Code's order after the type-time indentation, which now also inserts the closing text (`Result.AutoClosed`). `edit.AutoClosedTracker` keeps the closing characters as decorations and forgets a pair when a cursor leaves it (`Cursors.SetOnChange` tells it), and Backspace deletes a pair the editor typed. The view model, the settings, `Process.AutoClosing()` and the app wire it up.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/autoclose/autoclose.go`, `internal/core/edit/{autoclose,autoclosed,autoindent,command,cursor,delete,options,type}.go`, `internal/core/tokens/tokenizer.go`, `internal/core/indent/indenttest/indenttest.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{commands,indentation,options,viewmodel}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,process}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.6.4.
- **Tests:** ported from VS Code's `cursor.test.ts`: open parens default, before whitespace, brackets before whitespace with quotes never, configurable `autoCloseBefore`, auto-pairing disabled, auto-wrapping configurable (`autoSurround` in all four values, several selections), quote, multi-character (`begin`, `/**`, `autoClosingComments: never`), undo of a multi-character pair (#338693) and with auto-indentation, `beforeText` (three cases and the shorter pair), contained pairs (#72177), #55314, #27937, #61070, #132912 (with VS Code's test tokenizer ported), brackets balance (#6841, with a stand-in for the bracket index), #25658, #37315 (four cases), #118270, #78527, #85983, #78833, #53357, #84998, #11476, #206774, #41825, "all cursors do the same thing when deleting left"; from `indentation.test.ts` the parts that 4.15 had to leave out: #209802 (`{` on an Allman line) and #225916. Emetos's own: the parse functions and the maps of `core/autoclose`, `TypeIfInserting` on a real grammar, the tracker (follows the text, several lines, a cursor outside, clear), the change hook of the cursors, a line that is not cheap to tokenize, and, in the editor with the real Go and TypeScript grammars and configurations: close, type over, stop typing over once the cursor left, Backspace on a typed pair and on a pair that was there, a quote that begins and one that ends a string, `/**` and its undo, surround, and every setting; the options read from the settings.
- **Not ported:** the tests of the input method (`startComposition`, `compositionType`, `endComposition`: #15825, #90016, #26820, #144690, #144693, #82701, #2773 and the composition cases of the `beforeText` and `begin` tests), which need the interceptors of the end of a composition; the tests that depend on VS Code's decoration events (#205598, #78975 snippet edits).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the interceptors of an input method's commit (VS Code's `compositionEndWithInterceptors`) are not done: after a composition nothing is typed over, closed or surrounded. The bracket balance test of the ported #6841 uses a small stack-based stand-in for the bracket index (the index has its own tests, and the editor test uses the real one). The pairs of the language are read when the file is opened, like the indentation rules: a change of the language of the document does not reload them. The legacy `docComment` electric support and the electric characters of VS Code are not ported (no bundled language uses them). `cheapToTokenize` now asks the token source whether or not there are indentation rules.

## 4.18 Comments

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.18-comments
- **Summary:** line and block comments (SPEC §8.6.5). A new `core/comment` holds the comment tokens of a language (`lineComment` with `noIndent`, `blockComment`) and a registry-backed `Languages`. `edit.Commands` gets `ToggleLineComment`, `AddLineComment`, `RemoveLineComment` and `ToggleBlockComment`, ports of `LineCommentCommand` (the analysis of the lines, the insertion points that line up, the block comment fallback of a language with no line comment, the tracked selection with VS Code's stickiness and `deltaColumn`) and `BlockCommentCommand`, and of the multi-selection handling of `comment.ts` (selections sorted, a shared line commented once). The language is read from the token at the start of the first line, so embedded code uses its own tokens. Commands `editor.action.commentLine`, `addCommentLine`, `removeCommentLine` and `blockComment`, keys Ctrl+/, Ctrl+K Ctrl+C, Ctrl+K Ctrl+U and Ctrl+Shift+A, settings `editor.comments.insertSpace` and `editor.comments.ignoreEmptyLines`, and the wiring of the view model, `Process.Comments()` and the app.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/comment/comment.go`, `internal/core/edit/{comment,options}.go`, `internal/core/{command/builtin,keybinding/defaults,settings/builtin}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{commands,indentation,options,viewmodel}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,process}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.6.5.
- **Tests:** all the cases of `lineCommentCommand.test.ts` and `blockCommentCommand.test.ts`, 45 line cases and 28 block cases converted to Go tables by a script (the suites: line comment, ignoreEmptyLines false, line comment as block comment, as block comment 2; insertSpace false; the case-insensitive and fixed-column tokens), `_analyzeLines`, `_normalizeInsertionPoint` (13 cases) and the three cases in mixed modes (#24047 twice, #36173); Emetos's own: several selections (a shared line, toggling twice, two cursors on a line, add after remove), a language with nothing to comment with, a block comment with an end token inside; the comment package; in the editor with the real grammars: toggle over lines with undo, add and remove, block comment, the two settings, and Markdown with a Go fence (the fence gets `//`, the Markdown around it `<!-- -->`); the options read from the settings.
- **Not ported:** none (the mixed-mode tests were written by hand because they register tokenizers in VS Code's harness).

- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the commands run on the UI thread and read the tokens of the first line of a selection within the tokenization budget; a line that is not tokenized yet is read with the language of the document. Columns are bytes, as everywhere in Emetos; the comment tokens are compared by byte, which is the same as VS Code's for ASCII tokens (a token with a non-ASCII letter is case sensitive).

## 4.19 Folding model

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.19-folding-model
- **Summary:** `core/folding` (SPEC §8.7.1). `Regions` (VS Code's `FoldingRegions`: nested regions by first and last line with the parent of each, the folded flag, the source and the kind; `FindRange`; the packing of the parent index into the line arrays is not needed in Go), `SanitizeAndMerge` (the merge of the regions the provider finds with the folded and the user-made ones), the indentation provider `ComputeRanges`/`IndentProvider` (markers, `offSide`, the region limit that keeps the least indented regions, cancellable), the `Model` over a document (each region a decoration with the options a provider function gives, `Update`, `UpdatePost`, `ToggleCollapseState` with the hidden state of the regions inside a folded one, `RemoveManualRanges`, `Memento`/`ApplyMemento` with a checksum, `AllRegionsAtLine`, `RegionsInside`), the collapse functions (`SetCollapseStateLevelsDown`, `LevelsUp`, `Up`, `AtLevel`, `ForRest`, `ForMatchingLines`, `ForType`, `ToggleAt`) and the jumps (`ParentFoldLine`, `PreviousFoldLine`, `NextFoldLine`), the action-level commands (`Fold`, `Unfold`, `Toggle`, the recursive ones, `FoldAll`, `UnfoldAll`, `FoldLevel`, `FoldAllExcept`, `UnfoldAllExcept`, `FoldAllBlockComments`, `FoldAllMarkerRegions`, `UnfoldAllMarkerRegions`), and `Attach`, the background updater (a debounce of 200 ms, a goroutine on a snapshot, cancelled by a newer change, a result for an older text dropped, nothing in large file mode). `Document.SetDecorationOptions` changes the options of a decoration; `indent.Level` is the indentation of a line; the setting `editor.foldingMaximumRegions` (10 to 65,000, default 5,000). The package list of the SPEC now has `autoclose/` and `comment/`, which steps 4.17 and 4.18 had left out.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/folding/{regions,indent,model,commands,actions,provider}.go`, `internal/core/document/decorations.go`, `internal/core/indent/levels.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.7.1.
- **Tests:** ported from VS Code: all of `indentRangeProvider.test.ts` (the 33 calls of its 24 tests: one to three levels, decreasing indent, Java, Javadoc, whitespace in Java and Python, tabs, the 17 region cases, #35981, #79359, the markers with their flags), all of `foldingModel.test.ts` (getRegionAtLine, collapse, update, delete, getRegionsInside, with level, getRegionAtLine2, the set-collapse functions, the decorations, fold jumping, #129503 and the five `removeManualRanges` cases), and `foldingRanges.test.ts` (the maximum, `findRange`, `setCollapsed`, `sanitizeAndMerge` 1 to 4); the harness keeps VS Code's one-based lines. Emetos's own: the regions' text form and helpers, the route tests of `FoldingInvalidRegions`, `FoldingLineOutOfRange` and `FoldingCancelled`, the limit with its report, the merge keeping or unfolding a region by the cursor, the 19 action-level commands, the change events, folds following edits, the memento (restored, text changed, entries not regions), the updater (one computation for quick edits, stop, large file), `SetDecorationOptions`.
- **Not ported:** `hiddenRangeModel.test.ts` and `syntaxFold.test.ts` (the hidden ranges belong to the view, step 4.20; syntax ranges come from language servers, area 3), and `indentFold.test.ts` (it tests the provider through VS Code's editor harness; the same cases are in the ported `indentRangeProvider.test.ts` and in the model tests).
